\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil

-greedy over
F
if

\displaystyle\Delta(G_{j}\ominus(F\cup G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{j-1}))\geq\Delta(G_{l}\ominus(F\cup G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{j-1}))\text{ for all }1\leq j<l\leq m.

In the case
F=\emptyset
, we simply say that
(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})
is

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil

-greedy
.

Note that for any
F
and finite family
\mathcal{G}
of finite subgraphs of
\mathsf{Path}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, there exists an enumeration of
(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})
of
\mathcal{G}
which is

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil

-greedy with respect to
F
.
We next point out that if
(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})
is

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil

-greedy over
F
, then

\displaystyle\Delta(G_{1}\ominus F)\geq\Delta(G_{2}\ominus(F\cup G_{1}))\geq\Delta(G_{3}\ominus(F\cup G_{1}\cup G_{2}))\geq\dots\geq\Delta(G_{m}\ominus(F\cup G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{m-1})).

That is,

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil

-greediness implies that the sequence
(t_{1},\dots,t_{m})
is non-decreasing where
t_{j}=\Delta(G_{j}\ominus(F\cup G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{j-1}))
. However, this is not sufficient, since
\Delta
-greediness also requires that no transposition of two graphs in the sequence
G_{1},\dots,G_{m}
increases the value of
(t_{1},\dots,t_{m})
under the lexicographic order.

Example 4.2
.

Illustrated below is a graph
P\subset\mathsf{Path}_{\mathbb{Z}}
(on top) with
41
edges, formed by a union of edge-disjoint subgraphs
G_{1},\dots,G_{27}
.
The sequence
G_{1},\dots,G_{27}
is

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil

-greedy
and has
\Delta(G_{1},\dots,G_{27})=10
.
Graphs
G_{1},\dots,G_{7}
(below
P
) each have three edges, while
G_{8},\dots,G_{27}
each have a single edge.
The components of
\Delta(G_{j}\ominus(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{j-1}))
,
j\in\{1,\dots,7\}
, that contribute to
\Delta(G_{1},\dots,G_{27})
are circled.

To check that
(G_{1},\dots,G_{27})
is

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil

-greedy, it may be seen by inspection that

\displaystyle\vphantom{\big|}3

\displaystyle=\Delta(G_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\Delta(G_{j})

\displaystyle\text{for }2\leq j\leq 27,

\displaystyle 2

\displaystyle=\Delta(G_{2}\ominus G_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\Delta(G_{j}\ominus G_{1})

\displaystyle\text{for }3\leq j\leq 27,

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\Delta(G_{3}\ominus(G_{1}\cup G_{2}))

\displaystyle\geq\Delta(G_{j}\ominus(G_{1}\cup G_{2}))

\displaystyle\text{for }4\leq j\leq 27,

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\Delta(G_{4}\ominus(G_{1}\cup G_{2}\cup G_{3}))

\displaystyle\geq\Delta(G_{j}\ominus(G_{1}\cup G_{2}\cup G_{3}))

\displaystyle\text{for }5\leq j\leq 27,

and so on.

This example is the
t=3
case of a more general construction. For every positive integer
t
, there is a

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil

-greedy sequence
G_{1},\dots,G_{m}

with
\lambda(G_{1})=\dots=\lambda(G_{m})=1
and
\Delta(G_{1})=t
and

\displaystyle m

\displaystyle=t+(t+1)+(t+2)(t+1)

\displaystyle=t^{2}+5t+3,\vphantom{\bigg|}

\displaystyle\|G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{m}\|

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\|G_{j}\|=\underbrace{t+\dots+t}_{t\textup{ times}}\ \ +\ \ \underbrace{t+\dots+t}_{t+1\textup{ times}}\ \ +\ \ \underbrace{1+\dots+1}_{\!\!\!\!(t+2)(t+1)\textup{ times}\!\!\!\!}

\displaystyle=3t^{2}+4t+2,\vphantom{\big|}

\displaystyle\Delta(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})

\displaystyle=t+(t-1)+(t-2)+\dots+2+1\ \ +\ \ \underbrace{1+\dots+1}_{t+1\textup{ times}}\ \ +\ \ \underbrace{0+\dots+0}_{\!\!\!\!(t+2)(t+1)\textup{ times}\!\!\!\!}

\displaystyle=\frac{(t+2)(t+1)}{2}.\vphantom{\big|}\qquad\quad

As the following theorem shows, this construction achieves the maximum possible ratio of
\|G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{m}\|
to
\Delta(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})

for any given
t
.

Theorem 4.3
.

Suppose
G_{1},\dots,G_{m}
are graphs such that
\lambda(G_{1})=\dots=\lambda(G_{m})=1
and
(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})
is

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil

-greedy over
\emptyset
.
Let
t=\Delta(G_{1})
.
Then

\displaystyle\|G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{m}\|\leq\frac{2(3t^{2}+4t+2)}{(t+2)(t+1)}\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(G_{1},\dots,G_{m}).

Moreover,
this inequality is tight for all
t\geq 1
.

Note that Lemma
3.4
(I) follows directly from this theorem, since
\frac{2(3t^{2}+4t+2)}{(t+2)(t+1)}>6
for all
t\geq 1
.

Proof.

The theorem is proved by analyzing a linear program with
t+1
constraints and
C_{1}+\dots+C_{t+1}=\Theta(t^{-3/2}4^{t})
variables, where
C_{n}=\frac{1}{n+1}\binom{2n}{n}
is the
n
th Catalan number.
This linear program turns out to have an integral solution that corresponds to the construction given in Example
4.2
.

Without loss of generality, assume that for all
j\in[m]
, each edge of
G_{j}
has at most one endpoint in
V(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{j-1})
. In particular,
G_{1},\dots,G_{m}
are edge-disjoint and no two connected components of
G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{j-1}
belong to the same connected component of
G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{j}
.

For
j\in[m]
and
s\in\{0,\dots,t\}
, we say that a graph
G_{j}
has
level
s
if
\Delta(G_{j}\ominus(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{j-1}))=t-s
. In the sequence
G_{1},\dots,G_{m}
, the level
0
graphs come first, followed by level
1
, etc.
For a graph
G_{j}
of level
s
, we define the
profile
of
G_{j}
as the sequence
(a_{1},\dots,a_{s})\in\mathbb{N}^{s}
where
a_{r}
is the number of vertices that
G_{j}
shares with graphs of level
s-r
. For example, in Example
4.2
we have
t=3
and graphs
G_{1},G_{2},G_{3},G_{4}
have profiles
(),(1),(1,1),(1,1)
respectively.

For
s\in\{0,\dots,t\}
and
(a_{1},\dots,a_{s})\in\mathbb{N}^{s}
, let

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}

\displaystyle=\#\{\text{graphs of level $s$ with profile }(a_{1},\dots,a_{s})\}.

Note that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\ (t-s)\cdot\#\{\text{graphs of level }s\}=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}(t-s)\bm{w}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}},

\displaystyle\|G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{m}\|

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}(t-s+a_{1}+\dots+a_{s})\bm{w}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}.

s

Let
\gamma=\gamma(t)
be the constant

\displaystyle\gamma\vcentcolon=5+\frac{2}{t+1}-\frac{12}{t+2}\quad\bigg({=}\ \frac{2(3t^{2}+4t+2)}{(t+2)(t+1)}-1\bigg).

To prove the theorem (i.e., the inequality
\|G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{m}\|\leq(\gamma+1)\Delta(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})
), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{t}\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}(t-s+a_{1}+\dots+a_{s})\bm{w}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=0}^{t}\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}(\gamma+1)(t-s)\bm{w}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}.

The variables in our program will be indexed by Dyck sequences of length
\leq t
. We say that
(a_{1},\dots,a_{s})\in\mathbb{N}^{s}
is a
Dyck sequence
if
a_{1}+\dots+a_{r}\leq r
for all
r\in[s]
. (Note: The number of Dyck sequences of length
s
is the Catalan number
C_{s+1}
.)

For all
s\in\{0,\dots,t\}
and
(a_{1},\dots,a_{s})\in\mathbb{N}^{s}
, we claim that

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}\neq 0\

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\ (a_{1},\dots,a_{s})\text{ is a Dyck sequence}.

(1)

To see why, assume for contradiction that
\bm{w}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}\neq 0
and
a_{1}+\dots+a_{r}\geq r+1
for some
r\in[s]
.
Fix the
minimum
such
r
and
note that
a_{1}+\dots+a_{r-1}\leq r-1
and
a_{r}\geq 2
.
Since
\bm{w}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}\neq 0
, there exists
j\in[m]
such that
G_{j}
has level
s
and profile
(a_{1},\dots,a_{r})
.
Since
a_{r}
is positive, there exists at least at least
i\in[m]
such that
G_{i}
has level
s-r
.
Now fix the
minimum
such
i
and note that
i<j
. Observe that

\displaystyle\Delta(G_{j}\ominus(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{i-1}))

\displaystyle=\Delta(G_{j}\ominus(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{j-1}))+a_{1}+\dots+a_{r},

since each edge of
G_{j}
that contributes to the sum
a_{1}+\dots+a_{r}
(i.e., shares an endpoint with a graph of level between
s-1
and
s-r
) contributes
0
to
\Delta(G_{j}\ominus(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{i-1}))
and
1
to
\Delta(G_{j}\ominus(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{j-1}))
.
Since
a_{1}+\dots+a_{r}\geq r+1
, it follows that

\displaystyle\Delta(G_{j}\ominus(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{i-1}))

\displaystyle\geq\Delta(G_{j}\ominus(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{j-1}))+r+1

\displaystyle=t-s+r+1

\displaystyle>t-s+r

\displaystyle=\Delta(G_{i}\ominus(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{i-1})).

However, this contradicts
\Delta
-greediness of the sequence
(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})
, proving the implication (
1
).

We next observe
t+1
linear inequalities among the
\bm{w}
-numbers:

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{()}

\displaystyle\geq 1,

(
\ast_{0}
)

\displaystyle\qquad\sum_{s=r}^{t}\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}a_{s-r}\bm{w}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{r-1}}\Big(a_{1}+\dots+a_{r-1}+2(t-r+1)\Big)\bm{w}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{r-1}}\quad\text{ for }r\in[t].

(
\ast_{r}
)

Here constraint (
\ast_{0}
) expresses that “
\Delta(G_{1})\geq t
”. For
r\in[t]
, the constraint (
\ast_{r}
) expresses “the number of endpoints of graphs of level
\geq r
that match with endpoints of graph of level
r-1
is at most the number of
available
endpoints of level
s
” (i.e.,
2(t-r+1)
for each graph of level
r-1
, plus
1
for each edge of level
r-1
that has an endpoint in level
\leq r-2
).

Let us now forget the definition of numbers
\bm{w}_{\vec{a}}
in terms of graph
G_{1},\dots,G_{m}
and instead view
\bm{w}_{\vec{a}}
as a family of real-valued variables indexed by Dyck sequences of length
\leq t
.
Consider the linear program

\displaystyle\max_{\vphantom{\big|}\bm{w}_{\vec{a}}\,\in\,\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\textup{ for each Dyck sequence $\vec{a}$ of length $\leq t$}\,:\,(\ast_{0}),\dots,(\ast_{t})}\ \sum_{s=0}^{t}\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}\Big(a_{1}+\dots+a_{s}-(t-s)\gamma\Big)\bm{w}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}.

(LP)

Our goal is to show that this (LP) has value
0
.

In fact, an optimal solution is given by the following integer-valued
\bm{w}
-vector. Let
\vec{\alpha}_{0},\dots,\vec{\alpha}_{t}
be the following Dyck sequences of length
0,\dots,t
respectively:

\displaystyle\vec{\alpha}_{0}=(),\quad\vec{\alpha}_{1}=(1),\quad\vec{\alpha}_{2}=(1,1),\quad\cdots,\quad\vec{\alpha}_{t-1}=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{t-1}),\quad\vec{\alpha}_{t}=(1,\underbrace{0,\dots,0}_{t-1}).

Then
\bm{w}
is given by

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{0}}=\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{1}}=\dots=\bm{w}_{\alpha_{t-2}}

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{t-1}}

\displaystyle=t+1,

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle=(t+2)(t+1),

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{\vec{\beta}}

\displaystyle=0\quad\text{ for all }\vec{\beta}\notin\{\vec{\alpha}_{0},\dots,\vec{\alpha}_{k}\}.\quad

This vector corresponds to the greedy construction given in Example
4.2
. A simple calculation shows that (LP) has value
0
under this
\bm{w}
.

To prove optimality, we consider the dual linear program given by

\displaystyle\min_{\vphantom{\big|}\bm{y}_{0},\dots,\bm{y}_{t}\,\in\,\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,:\,(\star_{\vec{a}})\textup{ for each Dyck sequence $\vec{a}$ of length $\leq t$}}\ -\bm{y}_{0}

(dual LP)

where constraints (
\star_{\vec{a}}
) are as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{y}_{0}+2t\bm{y}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq t\gamma,

(
\star_{()}
)

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{t}a_{t-r}\bm{y}_{r}

\displaystyle\geq a_{1}+\dots+a_{t},

(
\star_{a_{1},\dots,a_{t}}
)

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{s}a_{s-r}\bm{y}_{r}-\Big(a_{1}+\dots+a_{s}+2(t-s)\Big)\bm{y}_{s+1}

\displaystyle\geq a_{1}+\dots+a_{s}-(t-s)\gamma\quad\text{ for }s\in[t-1].\qquad

(
\star_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}
)

We claim that
the following vector
\bm{y}=(\bm{y}_{0},\dots,\bm{y}_{t})\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{t+1}
is an optimal solution to the dual LP:

\displaystyle\bm{y}_{0}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\bm{y}_{r}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{(r-1)(4t+2-r)}{2(2t+2-r)(2t+1-r)}\quad\text{ for }r\in[t].

To see this, we first note that

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2}>\frac{\gamma}{2}=\bm{y}_{1}>\bm{y}_{2}>\dots>\bm{y}_{t}=1.

Next, note that constraint (
\star_{()}
) clearly holds with equality (i.e.,
\bm{y}_{0}+2t\bm{y}_{1}=0+2t\frac{\gamma}{2}=\gamma
).
To check that
\bm{y}
satisfies (
\star_{a_{1},\dots,a_{t}}
) for Dyck sequences of length
t
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{t}a_{t-r}\bm{y}_{r}-(a_{1}+\dots+a_{t})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{t}(\bm{y}_{t-s+1}-1)a_{s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{(t-s)(3t+s+1)}{2(t+s+1)(t+s)}-1\right)a_{s}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{(t-s)(3t+s+1)}{2(t+s+1)(t+s)}-1\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{t^{2}(t-1)}{(t+1)(t+2)}

\displaystyle\geq 0.

The first inequality above follows from Karamata’s inequality, using the fact that
(1,\dots,1)
majorizes
(a_{1},\dots,a_{t})
and the rational function

\displaystyle\rho(x)\vcentcolon=\frac{(t-x)(3t+x+1)}{2(t+x+1)(t+x)}

is convex over
x\in[0,t]
. (Karamata’s inequality implies that
\sum_{s=1}^{t}\rho(s)a_{s}\leq\sum_{s=1}^{t}\rho(s)
for every convex function
\rho
.) This shows that
\bm{y}
satisfies (
\star_{a_{1},\dots,a_{t}}
). The argument that
\bm{y}
satisfies (
\star_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}
) for each Dyck sequence of length
s<t
uses a similar application of Karamata’s inequality.

Finally,
note that the objective function
-\bm{y}_{0}
of the dual LP at the feasible point
\bm{y}
equals
0
. Therefore, both the primal and dual LPs have value
0
, which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.4
.

Optimality of
\bm{w}
and
\bm{y}
may be seen by considering
the primal LP in standard matrix form with columns restricted to the support of
\bm{w}
(i.e., with columns
\vec{\alpha}_{0},\dots,\vec{\alpha}_{t}
). This looks like

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{0}},\dots,\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{t}}\,\geq\,0}\ f^{\top}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{0}}\\
\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{1}}\\
\vdots\\
\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{t}}\end{array}\right)\quad\text{ subject to }\quad M\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{0}}\\
\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{1}}\\
\vdots\\
\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{t}}\end{array}\right)\leq\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-1\\
0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{array}\right)

where

\displaystyle f=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-t\gamma\\
1-(t-1)\gamma\\
2-(t-2)\gamma\\
\vdots\\
t-2-2\gamma\\
t-1-\gamma\\
1\end{array}\right),\qquad M=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}\ -1&0&\ \>0&\ \ \ \cdots&0&0&\ 0\\
-2t&1&\ \>1&\ \ \ \cdots&1&1&\ 0\\
0&-2t+1&\ \>1&\ \ \ \cdots&1&1&\ 0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ \>\vdots&\ \ \ \ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\ \vdots\\
0&0&\ \>0&\ \ \ \cdots&1&1&\ 0\\
0&0&\ \>0&\ \ \ \cdots&-t-2&1&\ 0\\
0&0&\ \>0&\ \ \ \cdots&0&-t-1&\ \,\ 1\end{array}\right).

Optimality of

\bm{w}
and
\bm{y}
is then certified by checking that

\displaystyle M\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{0}}\\
\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{1}}\\
\vdots\\
\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{t}}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-1\\
0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{array}\right),\qquad M^{\top}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{y}_{0}\\
\bm{y}_{1}\\
\vdots\\
\bm{y}_{t}\end{array}\right)=f,\qquad f^{\top}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{0}}\\
\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{1}}\\
\vdots\\
\bm{w}_{\vec{\alpha}_{t}}\end{array}\right)=-\bm{y}_{0}

\displaystyle=0.

Corollary 4.5
.

If
(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})
is

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil

-greedy over
F
, then

\displaystyle\frac{\|(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{m})\ominus F\|}{\max_{j\in[m]}\lambda(G_{j}\ominus F)}\leq 6\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(G_{1},\dots,G_{m}\mid F).

Proof.

Suppose
(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})
is

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil

-greedy over
F
. We can easily modify these graphs to obtains a sequence
(G_{1}^{\prime},\dots,G_{m}^{\prime})
which is

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil

-greedy over
\emptyset
and satisfies

\displaystyle\lambda(G_{1}^{\prime})=\dots=\lambda(G_{m}^{\prime})=1,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(G_{1}^{\prime},\dots,G_{m}^{\prime})=\Delta(G_{1},\dots,G_{m}\mid F),\vphantom{\Big|}

\displaystyle\|G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{m}\|\leq\|G_{1}^{\prime}\cup\dots\cup G_{m}^{\prime}\|\cdot\max_{j\in[m]}\lambda(G_{j}\ominus F).

The idea is simple: we form
G_{j}^{\prime}
by contracting each component of
G_{j}\ominus(F\cup G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{j-1})
that is contained in
(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{m})\ominus F
to a single edge.
The bound then follows from Theorem
4.3
.
∎

Corollary
4.5
is not exactly what we need for our proof of Lemma
3.5
(I) in the next subsection. Instead of a bound on
\|(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{m})\ominus F\|
, we require a bound on the potentially larger quantity
\|(G\ominus F)\cup\dots\cup(G_{m}\ominus F)\|
.
Fortunately, LP duality again yields a lower bound.

Lemma 4.6

(Strengthening of Lemma
3.4
(I))
.

If
(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})
is

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil

-greedy over
F
, then

\displaystyle\frac{\|(G_{1}\ominus F)\cup\dots\cup(G_{m}\ominus F)\|}{\max_{j\in[m]}\lambda(G_{j}\ominus F)}

\displaystyle\leq 5\Delta(F)+6\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(G_{1},\dots,G_{m}\mid F).

Proof.

We slightly modify the linear program considered in Theorem
4.3
.
For
t=\Delta(G_{1}\ominus F)
, we now index variables by sequences
(a_{1},\dots,a_{s};b)
where
a_{1},\dots,a_{s}
is a Dyck sequence of length
\leq t
and
b\in\{0,\dots,2\Delta(F)\}
(representing the number of endpoints of
F
that a given
G_{j}
covers).
In addition to (appropriately modified) constraints (
\ast_{0}
),…,(
\ast_{t}
), we introduce an extra constraint

\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}\,\sum_{b}\,\bm{w}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s};b}\leq 2\Delta(F).

(
\ast_{t+1}
)

We maximize essentially the same objective function as before:

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{t}\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}}\,\sum_{b}\,\Big(a_{1}+\dots+a_{s}-(t-s)\gamma\Big)\bm{w}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{s};b}

for the same constant
\gamma\vcentcolon=5+\frac{2}{t+1}-\frac{12}{t+2}
(
<5
). The value of this LP gives an upper bound on

\displaystyle(\gamma+1)\cdot\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(G_{1},\dots,G_{m}\mid F)-\frac{\|(G_{1}\ominus F)\cup\dots\cup(G_{m}\ominus F)\|}{\max_{j\in[m]}\lambda(G_{j}\ominus F)}.

It turns out that this value is
\gamma\cdot\Delta(F)
. However, unlike the LP of Theorem
4.3
(or this modified version in the case
\Delta(F)=0
), the primal optimal solution is no longer integral.
Nevertheless, we can show that
\gamma\cdot\Delta(F)
is an upper bound by considering the dual LP.
The dual optimal solution
\bm{y}=(\bm{y}_{0},\dots,\bm{y}_{t+1})
happens to coincide on
\bm{y}_{0},\dots,\bm{y}_{t}
with the dual optimal solution of Theorem
4.3
; the extra coordinate
\bm{y}_{t+1}
equals
\gamma/2
. We omit the calculations showing this, as the details are similar to Theorem
4.3
.
∎

4.2
Proof of Main Lemma
3.5
(I)

We shall now prove:

Main Lemma
3.5
(I)

(rephrased)
.

For every covering
\mathcal{G}
of
\mathsf{Path}_{k}
, there exists a sequence
G_{1},\dots,G_{m}
of graphs in
\mathcal{G}
(w.l.o.g. an enumeration of
\mathcal{G}
) such that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})\geq\frac{k}{30}.

Proof.

Let
r=\lceil\log(k+1)\rceil
. We greedily construct integers
m_{1},\dots,m_{r}\geq 0
and a sequence

\displaystyle G_{1,1},\dots,G_{1,m_{1}},G_{2,1},\dots,G_{2,m_{2}},\cdots,G_{r,1},\dots,G_{r,m_{r}}\in\mathcal{G}

such that, letting
F_{0}=\emptyset
and

\displaystyle\vphantom{\Big|}F_{i}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=F_{i-1}\cup G_{i,1}\cup\dots\cup G_{i,m_{i}},

\displaystyle G^{i,j}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=G_{i,j}\ominus(F_{i-1}\cup G_{i,1}\cup\dots\cup G_{i,j-1}),

the following properties hold for all
i\in[r]
and
j\in[m_{i}]
:

(i)

\lambda(G\ominus F_{i})<2^{r-i}

for all

G\in\mathcal{G}
,

(ii)

\lambda(G^{i,j})\geq 2^{r-i}
,

(iii)

\displaystyle\Delta(G^{i,j})=\max_{\vphantom{\big|}G\in\mathcal{G}\,:\,\lambda(G\,\ominus\,(F_{i-1}\,\cup\,G_{i,1}\,\cup\,\dots\,\cup\,G_{i,j-1}))\,\geq\,2^{r-i}}\Delta(G\ominus(F_{i-1}\cup G_{i,1}\cup\dots\cup G_{i,j-1}))
.

To see that such a sequence exists,
assume for
i\in[r]
and
j\geq 1
that we have constructed integers
m_{1},\dots,m_{i-1}
(but not yet
m_{i}
) and graphs
G_{1,1},\dots,G_{i,j-1}
(but not
G_{i,j}
) which so far satisfy properties (i), (ii), (iii). There are now two possibilities for the next step in the construction:

•

Suppose that there exists
G\in\mathcal{G}
such that
\lambda(G\ominus(F_{i-1}\cup G_{i,1}\cup\dots\cup G_{i,j-1}))\geq 2^{r-i}
.

In this case, we pick
G_{i,j}
to be any such
G
which maximizes
\Delta(G\ominus(F_{i-1}\cup G_{i,1}\cup\dots\cup G_{i,j-1}))
.

Note that properties (ii) and (iii) are satisfied with respect to
i
and
j
.

•

Suppose that for all
G\in\mathcal{G}
, we have
\lambda(G\ominus(F_{i-1}\cup G_{i,1}\cup\dots\cup G_{i,j-1}))<2^{r-i}
.

In this case, we set
m_{i}\vcentcolon=j-1
.

Note that property (i) is satisfied with respect to
i
.

For the base case
i=j=1
of this inductive procedure, note that
\lambda(G\ominus F_{0})=\lambda(G)\leq k<2^{r}
for all
G\in\mathcal{G}
.

Having constructed the sequence
G_{1,1},\dots,G_{r,m_{r}}
, we turn to showing that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(G_{1,1},\dots,G_{r,m_{r}})\ \bigg(\!{=}\ \sum_{i\in[r]}\sum_{j\in[m_{i}]}\lambda(G^{i,j})\Delta(G^{i,j})\bigg)\geq\frac{k}{30}.

Note that by property (ii), for all
i\in[r]
,

\displaystyle 2^{r-i}\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(G_{i,1},\dots,G_{i,m_{i}}\mid F_{i})

\displaystyle=2^{r-i}\sum_{j\in[m_{i}]}\Delta(G^{i,j})\leq\sum_{j\in[m_{i}]}\lambda(G^{i,j})\Delta(G^{i,j}).

We next prove two inequalities:

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[r]}2^{r-i}\Delta(F_{i})

\displaystyle\leq\phantom{2}2\sum_{i\in[r]}\sum_{j\in[m_{i}]}\lambda(G^{i,j})\Delta(G^{i,j}),

(10)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[r]}\Big\|\bigcup_{j\in[m_{i}]}(G_{i,j}\ominus F_{i-1})\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq 22\sum_{i\in[r]}\sum_{j\in[m_{i}]}\lambda(G^{i,j})\Delta(G^{i,j}).

(11)

For inequality (
10
), noting that
\emptyset=F_{0}\subseteq F_{1}\subseteq\dots\subseteq F_{r}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[r]}2^{r-i}\Delta(F_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in[r]}2^{r-i}\sum_{h\in[i]}\Delta(F_{h}\ominus F_{h-1})

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{i\in[r]}2^{r-i}\Delta(F_{i}\ominus F_{i-1})

\displaystyle=2\sum_{i\in[r]}2^{r-i}\Delta((G_{i,1}\cup\dots\cup G_{i,m_{i}})\ominus F_{i-1})

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{i\in[r]}2^{r-i}\Delta(G_{i,1},\dots,G_{i,m_{i}}\mid F_{i-1})

(by Lemma
2.5

(a))

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{i\in[r]}\sum_{j\in[m_{i}]}\lambda(G^{i,j})\Delta(G^{i,j})

\displaystyle\text{(by (\ref{eq:ld3}))}.

For inequality (
11
), note that condition (iii) implies that
(G_{i,1},\dots,G_{i,m_{i}})
is

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil

-greedy over
F_{i-1}
for all
i\in[r]
. Therefore, by Lemma
4.6
and property (i),

\displaystyle\Big\|\bigcup_{j\in[m_{i}]}(G_{i,j}\ominus F_{i-1})\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq 2^{r-i+1}\Big(5\Delta(F_{i-1})+6\Delta(G_{i,1},\dots,G_{i,m_{i}}\mid F_{i-1})\Big).

Inequality (
11
) now follows from (
4.2
) and (
10
).

In the next part of the argument, we consider the
2^{r-i+1}
-neighborhood
of each graph
F_{i}
.
To define this formally: for a finite graph
P\subset\mathsf{Path}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, let us write
\mathsf{Nbd}_{1}(P)
for the graph consisting of all edges
\mathsf{Path}_{\mathbb{Z}}
incident to a vertex of
P
.
For
t\in\mathbb{N}
, let

\displaystyle\mathsf{Nbd}_{t}(P)\vcentcolon=\underbrace{\mathsf{Nbd}_{1}(\ldots\mathsf{Nbd}_{1}}_{t\text{ times}}(P)\ldots).

Note the inequalities
\Delta(\mathsf{Nbd}_{t}(P))\leq\Delta(P)
and
\|\mathsf{Nbd}_{t}(P)\|\leq 2t\Delta(P)+\|P\|
.

For finite graphs
P,Q\subset\mathsf{Path}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, let
P\setminus Q
denote the graph with edge set
E(P)\setminus E(Q)
(and no isolated vertices).
Here is another obvious inequality, numbered for later reference:

\|\mathsf{Nbd}_{t}(P)\setminus Q\|\leq 2t\Delta(P)+\|P\setminus Q\|.

(12)

Note that
P\ominus Q\subseteq P\setminus Q
. In the other direction, note that
P\setminus Q_{1}\subseteq P\ominus Q_{2}
if and only if
(
C\subseteq Q_{1}
or
V(C)\cap V(Q_{2})=\emptyset
) for every connected component
C
of
P
.

Now comes a key claim: for all
i\in[r]
and
G\in\mathcal{G}
, we have

G\setminus\bigcup_{h\in[i]}\mathsf{Nbd}_{2^{r-h+1}}(F_{h})\subseteq G\ominus F_{i}.

(13)

To see why, suppose
C
is a connected component of
G
such that
V(C)\cap V(F_{i})
is nonempty.
We must show that
C\subseteq\bigcup_{h\in[i]}\mathsf{Nbd}_{2^{r-h+1}}(F_{h})
. There are two cases:

•

If
\|C\|<2^{r-i+1}
, then clearly
C\subseteq\mathsf{Nbd}_{2^{r-i+1}}(F_{i})
since
C
is connected and shares a vertex with
F_{i}
.

•

If
\|C\|\geq 2^{r-i+1}
, then we consider the unique
h\in[i-1]
such that
2^{r-h}\leq\|C\|<2^{r-h+1}
.
(Note that
h
is well-defined since
\|C\|\leq k<2^{r}
.)

By property (i), we have
\lambda(G\ominus F_{h})<2^{r-h}
. It follows that
V(C)\cap V(F_{h})
is nonempty, since otherwise
C\subseteq G\ominus F_{h}
and we would have a contradictory inequality
2^{r-h}\leq\|C\|\leq\lambda(G\ominus F_{h})<2^{r-h}
.
Since
C
is connected of length
<2^{r-h+1}
and shares a vertex with
F_{h}
, we conclude that
C\subseteq\mathsf{Nbd}_{2^{r-h+1}}(F_{h})
.

We next observe that for all
G\in\mathcal{G}
, case
i=r
of property (i) implies that
\lambda(G\ominus F_{r})<1
and hence
G\ominus F_{r}=\emptyset
. Case
i=r
of the containment (
13
) now yields

\displaystyle G\setminus\bigcup_{i\in[r]}\mathsf{Nbd}_{2^{r-i+1}}(F_{i})\subseteq G\ominus F_{r}=\emptyset,\quad\text{ that is, }\quad G\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in[r]}\mathsf{Nbd}_{2^{r-i+1}}(F_{i}).

Since
\bigcup_{G\in\mathcal{G}}G=\mathsf{Path}_{k}
, it follows that

\mathsf{Path}_{k}\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in[r]}\mathsf{Nbd}_{2^{r-i+1}}(F_{i}).

The proof now finishes as follows:

\displaystyle k

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\bigcup_{i\in[r]}\mathsf{Nbd}_{2^{r-i+1}}(F_{i})\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[r]}\Big\|\mathsf{Nbd}_{2^{r-i+1}}(F_{i})\setminus\bigcup_{h\in[i-1]}\mathsf{Nbd}_{2^{r-h+1}}(F_{h})\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[r]}\Big(2^{r-i+2}\Delta(F_{i})+\Big\|F_{i}\setminus\bigcup_{h\in[i-1]}\mathsf{Nbd}_{2^{r-h+1}}(F_{h})\Big\|\Big)

(by (
12
))

\displaystyle=4\sum_{i\in[r]}2^{r-i}\Delta(F_{i})+\sum_{i\in[r]}\Big\|\bigcup_{j\in[m_{j}]}\Big(G_{i,j}\setminus\bigcup_{h\in[i-1]}\mathsf{Nbd}_{2^{r-h+1}}(F_{h})\Big)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{i\in[r]}2^{r-i}\Delta(F_{i})+\sum_{i\in[r]}\Big\|\bigcup_{j\in[m_{j}]}(G_{i,j}\ominus F_{i})\Big\|

(by (
13
))

\displaystyle\leq 30\sum_{i\in[r]}\sum_{j\in[m_{i}]}\lambda(G^{i,j})\Delta(G^{i,j})

(by (
10
) and (
11
))

\displaystyle=30\,\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(G_{1,1},\dots,G_{r,m_{r}}).

4.3
Proof of Theorem
3.6
(I)

We will actually prove the following slightly more general version of Theorem
3.6
(I), whose statement is better suited to induction on
d
.

Main Theorem
3.6
(I)

(slightly more general restatement)
.

Let
P
be a finite subgraph of
\mathsf{Path}_{\mathbb{Z}}
and let
T
be a
P
-join tree of
\langle\!\hskip-0.7pt\langle\rangle\!\hskip-0.7pt\rangle
-depth
d
.
Then

\displaystyle\Psi(T)\geq\frac{1}{30\mathrm{e}}d\lambda(P)^{1/d}+\Delta(P)-d.

Proof.

We argue by induction on
d
. To clarify the exposition, we give headers to the various steps in the argument.

The base case
d=1

(We could actually treat
d=0
as the base case of the induction, but instead choose
d=1
since it will help to illustrate the reduction to the case
\Delta(P)=1
in the next step.)

For the base case
d=1
, let
\mathcal{G}
be the rightmost branch covering of
T
. Note that
\mathcal{G}
is the set of single-edge subgraphs of
P
.

The graph
P
is a disjoint union of paths
P_{1},\dots,P_{c}
where
c\vcentcolon=\Delta(P)
and
\lambda(P)=\|P_{1}\|
(without loss of generality).
Let
S_{1}
be a set consisting of
\lceil\lambda(P)/2\rceil
vertex-disjoint edges in
P_{1}
, and let
S_{2}
be a set containing one edge from each of
P_{2},\dots,P_{c}
. Let
e_{1},\dots,e_{m}
be any enumeration of the edges of
P
with respect to which the edges in
S_{1}\cup S_{2}
come before edges outside this set, and let
G_{1},\dots,G_{m}
be the corresponding enumeration of
\mathcal{G}
. We have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})\geq|S_{1}|+|S_{2}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda(P)+c=\frac{1}{2}d\lambda(P)^{1/d}+\Delta(P)-d,

which proves the base case since

1/2>1/30\mathrm{e}
.

Induction step: reduction to the case
\Delta(P)=1

For the induction step, let
d\geq 2
and assume that the theorem holds for
d-1
. We argue that it suffices to prove the induction step in the case that
\Delta(P)=1
. To see this, we again decompose
P
as a disjoint union of paths
P_{1},\dots,P_{c}
where
c\vcentcolon=\Delta(P)
and
\lambda(P)=\|P_{1}\|
.

Let
T_{1}
be the
P_{1}
-join tree obtained from
T
by relabeling to
\emptyset
(the empty graph) any leaf originally labeled by an edge of
P_{2}\cup\dots\cup P_{c}
.
Assume that we are given a
T_{1}
-branch covering
\mathcal{G}_{1}
of
P_{1}
and an enumeration
H_{1},\dots,H_{m}
of
\mathcal{G}_{1}
such that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(H_{1},\dots,H_{m})\geq\frac{1}{30\mathrm{e}}d\lambda(P_{1})^{1/d}-d+1.

Let
G_{1},\dots,G_{m}
be the graphs corresponding to
H_{1},\dots,H_{m}
in the original tree
T
, and let
\mathcal{G}=\{G_{1},\dots,G_{m}\}
. Note that
\mathcal{G}
is a
T
-branch covering of
P
. Also note that
H_{j}=G_{j}\cap P_{1}
for all
j\in[m]
.

Writing
G^{j}
for
G_{j}\ominus(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup G_{j-1})
and
H^{j}
for
H_{j}\ominus(G_{1}\cup\dots\cup H_{j-1})
, we next observe that

\displaystyle\Delta(G^{j})\geq\Delta(H^{j})+\#\{i\in\{2,\dots,c\}:j\textup{ is the first index such that }G^{j}\cap P_{i}\textup{ is nonempty}\}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\Psi(T)\geq\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})=\sum_{j\in[m]}\Delta(G^{j})\geq\sum_{j\in[m]}\Delta(H^{j})+c-1=\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(H_{1},\dots,H_{m})+\Delta(P)-1.

Therefore, we have

\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})\geq\frac{1}{30\mathrm{e}}d\lambda(P)^{1/d}+\Delta(P)-d

as required.

Having established that it suffices to prove the induction step in the case that
\Delta(P)=1
, let us proceed under the assumption
P
is connected. Without loss of generality, let
P=\mathsf{Path}_{k}
where
k=\lambda(P)
. Thus, we assume that
T
is a
\mathsf{Path}_{k}
-join tree of
\langle\!\hskip-0.7pt\langle\rangle\!\hskip-0.7pt\rangle
-depth
d
. Our goal is to find a
T
-branch covering
\mathcal{G}
of
\mathsf{Path}_{k}
together with an enumeration
G_{1},\dots,G_{m}
of
\mathcal{G}
satisfying

\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(G_{1},\dots,G_{m})\geq\frac{1}{30\mathrm{e}}dk^{1/d}-d+1.

(14)

The right spine of
T

As a first step, we consider the “right spine”

T=\langle\!\hskip-0.7pt\langle T_{1},\dots,T_{\ell}\rangle\!\hskip-0.7pt\rangle
where
T_{\ell}
is a leaf and each
T_{j}
is a
B_{j}
-join tree of
\langle\!\hskip-0.7pt\langle\rangle\!\hskip-0.7pt\rangle
-depth
d-1
for graphs
B_{1}\cup\dots\cup B_{\ell}=\mathsf{Path}_{\ell}
.
(Note:
\{B_{1},\dots,B_{\ell}\}
is not necessarily the
T
-branch covering of
\mathsf{Path}_{k}
that we will eventually produce.)

Applying our Main Lemma
3.5
(II)
to graphs
B_{1},\dots,B_{\ell}
, we obtain a permutation
\pi:[\ell]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}[\ell]
satisfying

\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\lambda\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(B_{\pi(1)},\dots,B_{\pi(\ell)})\geq\frac{k}{30}.

(15)

For
j\in[\ell]
, it will be convenient to write

\displaystyle B^{j}\vcentcolon=B_{j}\ominus(B_{\pi(1)}\cup\dots\cup B_{\pi(\pi^{-1}(j)-1)}).

(Note:
B^{j}
is not to be confused with
B_{j}\ominus(B_{1}\cup\dots\cup B_{j-1})
.)
Under this reindexing, inequality (
15
) is equivalent to

\sum_{j\in[\ell]}\lambda(B^{j})\Delta(B^{j})\geq\frac{k}{30}.

(16)

Graph sequences
G^{1}_{1},\dots,G^{j}_{m_{j}}

For each
j\in[\ell]
, let
T^{j}
be the
B^{j}
-join tree obtained from
T_{j}
by
relabeling to
\emptyset
(the empty graph) any leaf originally labeled by an edge of
B_{j}\setminus B^{j}
.
Note that
T^{j}
has
\langle\!\hskip-0.7pt\langle\rangle\!\hskip-0.7pt\rangle
-depth
d-1
, since relabeling does not increase
\langle\!\hskip-0.7pt\langle\rangle\!\hskip-0.7pt\rangle
-depth.

Applying the induction hypothesis to each
T^{j}
,
we obtain a
T^{j}
-branch covering
\mathcal{H}^{j}
of
B^{j}
and an enumeration
\smash{H^{j}_{1},\dots,H^{j}_{n_{j}}}
of
\mathcal{H}^{j}
such that

\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\Delta\hfil$\crcr}}}(H^{j}_{1},\dots,H^{j}_{n_{j}})\geq\frac{1}{30\mathrm{e}}(d-1)\lambda(B^{j})^{1/(d-1)}+\Delta(B^{j})-d+1.

(17)

We now define a sequence of graph
G^{j}_{1},\dots,G^{j}_{m_{j}}
where
m_{j}=n_{j}+j-1

via the three bullet points below.
(See the diagram following the definition for a helpful example.)

•

Note that
\pi^{-1}(1),\dots,\pi^{-1}(j)
are
j
distinct numbers in
[m]
.

Let
\tau_{j}:[j]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}[j]
be the permutation defined by
\tau_{j}^{-1}(a)<\tau_{j}^{-1}(b)
iff
\pi^{-1}(a)<\pi^{-1}(b)
for all
a,b\in[j]
.

For example, if
j=4
and
\pi^{-1}(1,2,3,4)=(4,7,2,6)
, then
\tau_{j}^{-1}(1,2,3,4)=(2,4,1,3)
and
\tau_{j}(1,2,3,4)=(3,1,4,2)
.

•

Let
j^{\star}=\tau_{j}^{-1}(j)\in[j]
.

In our example,
j^{\star}=3
.

•

Let
G^{j}_{1},\dots,G^{j}_{m_{j}}
be the sequence

\displaystyle B_{\tau_{j}(1)},\dots,B_{\tau_{j}(j^{\star}-1)},H^{j}_{1},\dots,H^{j}_{n_{j}},B_{\tau_{j}(j^{\star}+1)},\dots,B_{\tau_{j}(j)}.

Said differently:
G^{j}_{1},\dots,G^{j}_{m_{j}}
is obtained from the sequence
B_{\tau_{j}(1)},\dots,B_{\tau_{j}(j)}
by removing the
j^{\ast}
-th entry (i.e., the graph
B_{\tau_{j}(j^{\star})}=B_{j}
) and inserting the subsequence
H^{j}_{1},\dots,H^{j}_{n_{j}}
in its place.

In our example (with
j=4
and
j^{\star}=3
),
G^{j}_{1},\dots,G^{j}_{m_{j}}
is the sequence
B_{3},B_{1},H^{4}_{1},\dots,H^{4}_{n_{4}},B_{2}
.

\begin{aligned} \vphantom{\big|}\pi:(1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8)&\mapsto(7,3,5,1,8,4,2,6)\\
\vphantom{\big|}\pi^{-1}:(1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8)&\mapsto(4,7,2,6,3,8,1,5)\\
\vphantom{\big|}\tau_{4}:(1,2,3,4)\phantom{5,,6,7,8}&\mapsto(2,4,1,3)\end{aligned}
\framebox{$G^{4}_{1},\dots,G^{4}_{10}$}=\framebox{$B_{3},B_{1},H^{4}_{1},\dots,H^{4}_{7},B_{2}$}

\mathsf{Path}_{k}

The

-branch covering
